val\\n785: \\t}\\n786: \\n787: \\treturn ret, diags\\n788: }\\n789: \\n790: func (d *evaluationStateData) getEphemeralResource(addr addrs.Resource, rng tfdiags.SourceRange) (cty.Value, tfdiags.Diagnostics) {\\n791: \\tvar diags tfdiags.Diagnostics\\n792: \\n793: \\tif d.Operation == walkValidate || d.Operation == walkEval {\\n794: \\t\\t// Ephemeral instances are never live during // Evaluator provides the necessary contextual data for evaluating expressions\n30: // for a particular walk operation.\n31: type Evaluator struct {\n32: \t// Operation defines what type of operation this evaluator is being used\n33: \t// for.\n34: \tOperation walkOperation\n35: \n36: \t// Meta is contextual metadata about the current operation.\n37: \tMeta *ContextMeta\n38: \n39: \t// Config is the root node in the configuration tree.\n40: \tConfig *configs.Config\n41: \n42: \t// Instances tracks the dynamic instances that are associated with each\n43: \t// module call or resource. The graph walk gradually registers the\n44: \t// set of instances for each object within the graph nodes for those\n45: \t// objects, and so as long as the graph has been built correctly the\n46: \t// set of instances for an object should always be available by the time\n47: \t// we're evaluating expressions that refer to it.\n48: \tInstances *instances.Expander\n49: \n50: \t// NamedValues is where we keep the values of already-evaluated input\n51: \t// variables, local values, and output values.\n52: \tNamedValues *namedvals.State\n53: \n54: \t// EphemeralResources tracks the currently-open instances of any ephemeral\n55: \t// resources.\n56: \tEphemeralResources *ephemeral.Resources\n57: \n58: \t// Deferrals tracks resources and modules that have had either their\n59: \t// expansion or their specific planned actions deferred to a future\n60: \t// plan/apply round.\n61: \tDeferrals *deferring.Deferred\n62: \n63: \t// Plugins is the library of available plugin components (providers and\n64: \t// provisioners) that we have available to help us evaluate expressions\n65: \t// that interact with plugin-provided objects.\n66: \t//\n67: \t// From this we only access the schemas of the plugins, and don't otherwise\n68: \t// interact with plugin instances.\n69: \tPlugins *contextPlugins\n70: \n71: \t// State is the current state, embedded in a wrapper that ensures that\n72: \t// it can be safely accessed and modified concurrently.\n73: \tState *states.SyncState\n74: \n75: \t// Changes is the set of proposed changes, embedded in a wrapper that\n76: \t// ensures they can be safely accessed and modified concurrently.\n77: \tChanges *plans.ChangesSync\n78: \n79: \tPlanTimestamp time.Time\n80: }\n81: \n82: // Scope creates an evaluation scope for the given module path and optional\n83: // resource.\n84: //\n85: // If the \"self\" argument is nil then the \"self\" object is not available\n86: // in evaluated expressions. \t\tfor k := range moduleConfig.Module.Variables {\n250: \t\t\tsuggestions = append(suggestions, k)\n251: \t\t}\n252: \t\tsuggestion := didyoumean.NameSuggestion(addr.Name, suggestions)\n253: \t\tif suggestion != \"\" {\n254: \t\t\tsuggestion = fmt.Sprintf(\" Did you mean %q?\", suggestion)\n255: \t\t} else {\n256: \t\t\tsuggestion = fmt.Sprintf(\" This variable can be declared with a variable %q {} block.\", addr.Name)\n257: \t\t}\n258: \n259: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n260: \t\t\tSeverity: hcl.DiagError,\n261: \t\t\tSummary:  `Reference to undeclared input variable`,\n262: \t\t\tDetail:   fmt.Sprintf(`An input variable with the name %q has not been declared.%s`, addr.Name, suggestion),\n263: \t\t\tSubject:  rng.ToHCL().Ptr(),\n264: \t\t})\n265: \t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n266: \t}\n267: \n268: \t// During the validate walk, input variables are always unknown so\n269: \t// that we are validating the configuration for all possible input values\n270: \t// rather than for a specific set. Checking against a specific set of\n271: \t// input values then happens during the plan walk.\n272: \t//\n273: \t// This is important because otherwise the validation walk will tend to be\n274: \t// overly strict, requiring expressions throughout the configuration to\n275: \t// be complicated to accommodate all possible inputs, whereas returning\n276: \t// unknown here allows for simpler patterns like using input values as\n277: \t// guards to broadly enable/disable resources, avoid processing things\n278: \t// that are disabled, etc. Terraform's static validation leans towards\n279: \t// being liberal in what it accepts because the subsequent plan walk has\n280: \t// more information available and so can be more conservative.\n281: \tif d.Operation == walkValidate {\n282: \t\t// We should still capture the statically-configured marks during\n283: \t\t// the validate walk.\n284: \t\tret := cty.UnknownVal(config.Type)\n285: \t\tif config.Sensitive {\n286: \t\t\tret = ret.Mark(marks.Sensitive)\n287: \t\t}\n288: \t\tif config.Ephemeral {\n289: \t\t\tret = ret.Mark(marks.Ephemeral)\n290: \t\t}\n291: \t\treturn ret, diags\n292: \t}\n293: \n294: \tval := d.Evaluator.NamedValues.GetInputVariableValue(d.ModulePath.InputVariable(addr.Name))\n295: \n296: \t// Mark if sensitive and/or ephemeral\n297: \tif config.Sensitive {\n298: \t\tval = val.Mark(marks.Sensitive)\n299: \t}\n300: \tif config.Ephemeral {\n301: \t\tval = val.Mark(marks.Ephemeral)\n302: \t}\n303: \n304: \treturn val, diags\n305: }\n306: \n307: func (d *evaluationStateData) GetLocalValue(addr addrs.LocalValue, rng tfdiags.SourceRange) (cty.Value, tfdiags.Diagnostics) {\n308: \tvar diags tfdiags.Diagnostics\n309: \n310: \t// First we'll make sure the requested value is declared in configuration,\n311: \t// so we can produce a nice message if not.\n312: \tmoduleConfig := d.Evaluator.Config.DescendantForInstance(d.ModulePath)\n313: \tif moduleConfig == nil {\n314: \t\t// should never happen, since we can't be evaluating in a module\n315: \t\t// that wasn't mentioned in configuration.\n316: \t\tpanic(fmt.Sprintf(\"local value read from %s, which has no configuration\", d.ModulePath))\n317: \t}\n318: \n319: \tconfig := moduleConfig.Module.Locals[addr.Name]\n320: \tif config == nil {\n321: \t\tvar suggestions []string\n322: \t\tfor k := range {\n327: \t\t\tsuggestion = fmt.Sprintf(\" Did you mean %q?\", suggestion)\n328: \t\t}\n329: \n330: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n331: \t\t\tSeverity: hcl.DiagError,\n332: \t\t\tSummary: rng.ToHCL().Ptr(),\n335: \t\t})\n336: \t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n337: \t}\n338: \n339: \tval := d.Evaluator.NamedValues.GetLocalValue(addr.Absolute(d.ModulePath))\n340: \treturn val, diags\n341: }\n342: \n343: func (d *evaluationStateData) GetModule(addr addrs.ModuleCall, rng tfdiags.SourceRange) (cty.Value, tfdiags.Diagnostics) {\n344: \tvar diags tfdiags.Diagnostics\n345: \n346: \t// Output results live in the module that declares them, which is one of\n347: \t// the child module instances of our current module path.\n348: \tmoduleAddr := d.ModulePath.Module().Child(addr.Name)\n349: \tabsAddr := addr.Absolute(d.ModulePath)\n350: \n351: \tparentCfg := d.Evaluator.Config.DescendantForInstance(d.ModulePath)\n352: \tcallConfig, ok := parentCfg.Module.ModuleCalls[addr.Name]\n353: \tif !ok {\n354: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n355: \t\t\tSeverity: hcl.DiagError,\n356: \t\t\tSummary:  `Reference to undeclared module`,\n357: \t\t\tDetail:   fmt.Sprintf(`The configuration contains no %s.`, moduleAddr),\n358: \t\t\tSubject:  rng.ToHCL().Ptr(),\n359: \t\t})\n360: \t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n361: \t}\n362: \n363: \t// We'll consult the configuration to see what output names we are\n364: \t// expecting, so we can ensure the resulting object is of the expected\n365: \t// type even if our data is incomplete for some reason.\n366: \tmoduleConfig := d.Evaluator.Config.Descendant(moduleAddr)\n367: \tif moduleConfig == nil {\n368: \t\t// should never happen, since we have a valid module call above, this\n369: \t\t// should be caught during static validation.\n370: \t\tpanic(fmt.Sprintf(\"output value read from %s, which has no configuration\", moduleAddr))\n371: \t}\n372: \toutputConfigs := moduleConfig.Module.Outputs\n373: \n374: \t// We don't do instance expansion during validation, and so we need to\n375: \t// return an unknown value. Technically we should always return\n376: \t// cty.DynamicVal here because the final value during plan will always\n377: \t// be an object or tuple type with unpredictable attributes/elements,\n378: \t// but because we never actually carry values forward from validation to\n379: \t// planning we lie a little here and return unknown list and map types,\n380: \t// just to give us more opportunities to catch author mistakes during\n381: \t// validation.\n382: \t//\n383: \t// This means that in practice any expression that refers to a module\n384: \t// call must be written to be valid for either a collection type or\n385: \t// structural type of similar kind, so that it can be considered as\n386: \t// valid during both the validate and plan walks.\n387: \tif d.Operation == walkValidate {\n388: \t\tatys := make(map[string]cty.Type, len(outputConfigs))\n389: \t\tfor name := range outputConfigs {\n390: \t\t\tatys[name] = cty.DynamicPseudoType // output values are dynamically-typed\n391: \t\t}\n392: \t\tinstTy := cty.Object(atys)\n393: \n394: \t\tswitch {\n395: \t\tcase callConfig.Count != nil:\n396: \t\t\treturn cty.UnknownVal(cty.List(instTy)), diags\n397: \t\tcase callConfig.ForEach != nil:\n398: \t\t\treturn cty.UnknownVal(cty.Map(instTy)), diags\n399: \t\tdefault:\n400: \t\t\treturn cty.UnknownVal(instTy), diags\n401: \t\t}\n402: \t}\n403: \n404: \t// For all other walk types, we proceed to dynamic evaluation of individual\n405: \t// instances, using the global instance expander. An earlier graph node\n406: \t// should always have registered the expansion of this module call before\n407: \t// we get here, unless there's a bug in the graph builders.\n408: \tallInstances := d.Evaluator.Instances\n409: \tinstKeyType, instKeys, known := allInstances.ExpandAbsModuleCall(absAddr)\n410: \tif !known {\n411: \t\t// If we don't know which instances exist then we can't really predict\n412: \t\t// anything at all. We can't even predict the return type based on\n413: \t\t// instKeyType because output values are dynamically-typed and so\n414: \t\t// our final result will always be an object or tuple type whose\n415: \t\t// attribute/element count we cannot predict.\n416: \t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n417: \t}\n418: \n419: \tinstanceObjVal := func(instKey addrs.InstanceKey) (cty.Value, tfdiags.Diagnostics) {\n420: \t\t// This function must always return a valid value, even if it's\n421: \t\t// just a cty.DynamicVal placeholder accompanying error diagnostics.\n422: \t\tvar diags tfdiags.Diagnostics\n423: \n424: \t\tnamedVals := d.Evaluator.NamedValues\n425: \t\tmoduleInstAddr := absAddr.Instance(instKey)\n426: \t\tattrs := make(map[string]cty.Value, len(outputConfigs))\n427: \t\tfor name := range outputConfigs {\n428: \t\t\toutputAddr := moduleInstAddr.OutputValue(name)\n429: \n430: \t\t\t// Although we do typically expect the graph dependencies to\n431: \t\t\t// ensure that values get registered before they are needed,\n432: \t\t\t// we track depedencies with specific output values where\n433: \t\t\t// possible, instead of with entire module calls, and so\n434: \t\t\t// in this specific case it's valid for some of this call's\n435: \t\t\t// output values to not be known yet, with the graph builder\n436: \t\t\t// being responsible for making sure that no expression\n437: \t\t\t// in the configuration can actually observe that.\n438: \t\t\tif !namedVals.HasOutputValue(outputAddr) {\n439: \t\t\t\tattrs[name] = cty.DynamicVal\n440: \t\t\t\tcontinue\n441: \t\t\t}\n442: \t\t\toutputVal := namedVals.GetOutputValue(outputAddr)\n443: \t\t\tattrs[name] = outputVal\n444: \t\t}\n445: \n446: \t\treturn cty.ObjectVal(attrs), diags\n447: \t}\n448: \n449: \tswitch instKeyType {\n450: \n451: \tcase addrs.NoKeyType:\n452: \t\t// In this case we should always have exactly one instance that\n453: \t\t// is addrs.NoKey. If not then there's a bug in the [instances.Expander]\n454: \t\t// implementation.\n455: \t\tif len(instKeys) != 1 {\n456: \t\t\tpanic(fmt.Sprintf(\"module call has no instance key type but has %d instances (should be 1)\", len(instKeys)))\n457: \t\t}\n458: \t\tret, moreDiags := instanceObjVal(instKeys[0])\n459: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(moreDiags)\n460: \t\treturn ret, diags\n461: \n462: \tcase addrs.IntKeyType:\n463: \t\t// We can assume that the instance keys are in ascending numerical order\n464: \t\t// and are consecutive, per the contract of allInstances.ExpandModuleCall.\n465: \t\telems := make([]cty.Value, 0, len(instKeys))\n466: \t\tfor _, instKey := range instKeys {\n467: \t\t\tinstVal, moreDiags := instanceObjVal(instKey)\n468: \t\t\telems = append(elems, instVal)\n469: \t\t\tdiags = diags.Append(moreDiags)\n470: \t\t}\n471: \t\treturn cty.TupleVal(elems), diags\n472: \n473: \tcase addrs.StringKeyType:\n474: \t\tattrs := make(map[string]cty.Value, len(instKeys))\n475: \t\tfor _, instKey := range instKeys {\n476: \t\t\tinstVal, moreDiags := instanceObjVal(instKey)\n477: \t\t\tattrs[string(instKey.(addrs.StringKey))] = instVal\n478: \t\t\tdiags = diags.Append(moreDiags)\n479: \t\t}\n480: \t\treturn cty.ObjectVal(attrs), diags\n481: \n482: \tdefault:\n483: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n484: \t\t\tSeverity: hcl.DiagError,\n485: \t\t\tSummary:  `Unsupported instance key type`,\n486: \t\t\tDetail: fmt.Sprintf(\n487: \t\t\t\t`Module call %s has instance key type %#v, which is not supported by the expression evaluator. This is a bug in Terraform.`,\n488: \t\t\t\tabsAddr, instKeyType,\n489: \t\t\t),\n490: \t\t\tSubject: rng.ToHCL().Ptr(),\n491: \t\t})\n492: \t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n493: \t}\n494: }\n495: \n496: func (d *evaluationStateData) GetResource(addr addrs.Resource, rng tfdiags.SourceRange) (cty.Value, tfdiags.Diagnostics) {\n497: \tvar diags tfdiags.Diagnostics\n498: \t// First we'll consult the configuration to see if an resource of this\n499: \t// name is declared at all.\n500: \tmoduleAddr := d.ModulePath\n501: \tmoduleConfig := d.Evaluator.Config.DescendantForInstance(moduleAddr)\n502: \tif moduleConfig == nil {\n503: \t\t// should never happen, since we can't be evaluating in a module\n504: \t\t// that wasn't mentioned in configuration.\n505: \t\tpanic(fmt.Sprintf(\"resource value read from %s, which has no configuration\", moduleAddr))\n506: \t}\n507: \n508: \tconfig := moduleConfig.Module.ResourceByAddr(addr)\n509: \tif config == nil {\n510: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n511: \t\t\tSeverity: hcl.DiagError,\n512: \t\t\tSummary:  `Reference to undeclared resource`,\n513: \t\t\tDetail:   fmt.Sprintf(`A resource %q %q has not been declared in %s`, addr.Type, addr.Name, moduleDisplayAddr(moduleAddr)),\n514: \t\t\tSubject:  rng.ToHCL().Ptr(),\n515: \t\t})\n516: \t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n517: \t}\n518: \n519: \t// Much of this function was written before we had factored out the handling\n520: \t// of instance keys into the separate instance expander model, and so it\n521: \t// does a bunch of instance-related work itself below.\n522: \t//\n523: \t// Currently, unknown instance keys are only possible when planning with\n524: \t// DeferralAllowed set to true in the PlanOpts, which should only be the\n525: \t// case in the stacks runtime (not the \"normal terraform\" modules runtime).\n526: \t// Thus, we have some amount of duplicated code remaining, to be more\n527: \t// certain that stacks-specific behaviors won't leak out into the standard\n528: \t// runtime.\n529: \t//\n530: \t// TODO: When deferred actions are more stable and robust in stacks, it\n531: \t// would be nice to rework this function to rely on the ResourceInstanceKeys\n532: \t// result for _all_ of its work, rather than continuing to duplicate a bunch\n533: \t// of the logic we've tried to encapsulate over ther already.\n534: \tif d.Operation == walkPlan || d.Operation == walkApply {\n535: \t\tif _, _, hasUnknownKeys := d.Evaluator.Instances.ResourceInstanceKeys(addr.Absolute(moduleAddr)); hasUnknownKeys {\n536: \t\t\t// There really isn't anything interesting we can do in this situation,\n537: \t\t\t// because it means we have an unknown for_each/count, in which case\n538: \t\t\t// we can't even predict what the result type will be because it\n539: \t\t\t// would be either an object or tuple type decided based on the instance\n540: \t\t\t// keys.\n541: \t\t\t// (We can't get in here for a single-instance resource because in that\n542: \t\t\t// case we would know that there's only one key and it's addrs.NoKey,\n543: \t\t\t// so we'll fall through to the other logic below.)\n544: \t\t\tunknownVal := cty.DynamicVal\n545: \n546: \t\t\t// If an ephemeral resource is deferred we need to mark the returned unknown value as ephemeral\n547: \t\t\tif addr.Mode == addrs.EphemeralResourceMode {\n548: \t\t\t\tunknownVal = unknownVal.Mark(marks.Ephemeral)\n549: \t\t\t}\n550: \t\t\treturn unknownVal, diags\n551: \t\t}\n552: \t}\n553: \n554: \t// Build the provider address from configuration, since we may not have\n555: \t// state available in all cases.\n556: \t// We need to build an abs provider address, but we can use a default\n557: \t// instance since we're only interested in the schema.\n558: \tschema := d.getResourceSchema(addr, config.Provider)\n559: \tif schema == nil {\n560: \t\t// This shouldn't happen, since validation before we get here should've\n561: \t\t// taken care of it, but we'll show a reasonable error message anyway.\n562: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n563: \t\t\tSeverity: hcl.DiagError,\n564: \t\t\tSummary:  `Missing resource type schema`,\n565: \t\t\tDetail:   fmt.Sprintf(\"No schema is available for %s in %s. This is a bug in Terraform and should be reported.\", addr, config.Provider),\n566: \t\t\tSubject:  rng.ToHCL().Ptr(),\n567: \t\t})\n568: \t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n569: \t}\n570: \tty := schema.ImpliedType()\n571: \n572: \tif addr.Mode == addrs.EphemeralResourceMode {\n573: \t\t// FIXME: This does not yet work with deferrals, and it would be nice to\n574: \t\t// find some way to refactor this so that the following code is not so\n575: \t\t// tethered to the current implementation details. Instead we should\n576: \t\t// have an abstract idea of first determining what instances the\n577: \t\t// resource has (using d.Evaluator.Instances.ResourceInstanceKeys) and\n578: \t\t// then retrieving the value for each instance to assemble into the\n579: \t\t// result, using some per-resource-mode logic maintained elsewhere.\n580: \t\treturn d.getEphemeralResource(addr, rng)\n581: \t}\n582: \n583: \trs := d.Evaluator.State.Resource(addr.Absolute(d.ModulePath))\n584: \n585: \tif rs == nil {\n586: \t\tswitch d.Operation {\n587: \t\tcase walkPlan, walkApply:\n588: \t\t\t// During plan and apply as we evaluate each removed instance they\n589: \t\t\t// are removed from the working state. Since we know there are no\n590: \t\t\t// instances, return an empty container of the expected type.\n591: \t\t\tswitch {\n592: \t\t\tcase config.Count != nil:\n593: \t\t\t\treturn cty.EmptyTupleVal, diags\n594: \t\t\tcase config.ForEach != nil:\n595: \t\t\t\treturn cty.EmptyObjectVal, diags\n596: \t\t\tdefault:\n597: \t\t\t\t// While we can reference an expanded resource with 0\n598: \t\t\t\t// instances, we cannot reference instances that do not exist.\n599: \t\t\t\t// Due to the fact that we may have direct references to\n600: \t\t\t\t// instances that may end up in a root output during destroy\n601: \t\t\t\t// (since a planned destroy cannot yet remove root outputs), we\n602: \t\t\t\t// need to return a dynamic value here to allow evaluation to\n603: \t\t\t\t// continue.\n604: \t\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"[ERROR] unknown instance %q referenced during %s\", addr.Absolute(d.ModulePath), d.Operation)\n605: \t\t\t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n606: \t\t\t}\n607: \n608: \t\tcase walkImport:\n609: \t\t\t// Import does not yet plan resource changes, so new resources from\n610: \t\t\t// config are not going to be found here. Once walkImport fully\n611: \t\t\t// plans resources, this case should not longer be needed.\n612: \t\t\t// In the single instance case, we can return a typed unknown value\n613: \t\t\t// for the instance to better satisfy other expressions using the\n614: \t\t\t// value. This of course will not help if statically known\n615: \t\t\t// attributes are expected to be known elsewhere, but reduces the\n616: \t\t\t// number of problematic configs for now.\n617: \t\t\t// Unlike in plan and apply above we can't be sure the count or\n618: \t\t\t// for_each instances are empty, so we return a DynamicVal. We\n619: \t\t\t// don't really have a good value to return otherwise -- empty\n620: \t\t\t// values will fail for direct index expressions, and unknown\n621: \t\t\t// Lists and Maps could fail in some type unifications.\n622: \t\t\tswitch {\n623: \t\t\tcase config.Count != nil:\n624: \t\t\t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n625: \t\t\tcase config.ForEach != nil:\n626: \t\t\t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n627: \t\t\tdefault:\n628: \t\t\t\treturn cty.UnknownVal(ty), diags\n629: \t\t\t}\n630: \n631: \t\tdefault:\n632: \t\t\t// We should only end up here during the validate walk (or\n633: \t\t\t// console/eval), since later walks should have at least partial\n634: \t\t\t// states populated for all resources in the configuration.\n635: \t\t\treturn cty.DynamicVal, diags\n636: \t\t}\n637: \t}\n638: \n639: \t// Now, we're going to build up a value that represents the resource\n640: \t// or resources that are in the state.\n641: \tinstances := map[addrs.InstanceKey]cty.Value{}\n642: \n643: \t// First, we're going to load any instances that we have written into the\n644: \t// deferrals system. A deferred resource overrides anything that might be\n645: \t// in the state for the resource, so we do this first.\n646: \tfor key, value := range d.Evaluator.Deferrals.GetDeferredResourceInstances(addr.Absolute(d.ModulePath)) {\n647: \t\tinstances[key] = value\n648: \t}\n649: \n650: \t// Decode all instances in the current state\n651: \tpendingDestroy := d.Operation == walkDestroy\n652: \tfor key, is := range rs.Instances {\n653: \t\tif _, ok := instances[key]; ok {\n654: \t\t\t// Then we've already loaded this instance from the deferrals so\n655: \t\t\t// we'll just ignore it being in state.\n656: \t\t\tcontinue\n657: \t\t}\n658: \t\t// Otherwise, we'll load the instance from state.\n659: \n660: \t\tif is == nil || is.Current == nil {\n661: \t\t\t// Assume we're dealing with an instance that hasn't been created yet.\n662: \t\t\tinstances[key] = cty.UnknownVal(ty)\n663: \t\t\tcontinue\n664: \t\t}\n665: \n666: \t\tinstAddr := addr.Instance(key).Absolute(d.ModulePath)\n667: \t\tchange := d.Evaluator.Changes.GetResourceInstanceChange(instAddr, addrs.NotDeposed)\n668: \t\tif change != nil {\n669: \t\t\t// Don't take any resources that are yet to be deleted into account.\n670: \t\t\t// If the referenced resource is CreateBeforeDestroy, then orphaned\n671: \t\t\t// instances will be in the state, as they are not destroyed until\n672: \t\t\t// after their dependants are updated.\n673: \t\t\tif change.Action == plans.Delete {\n674: \t\t\t\tif !pendingDestroy {\n675: \t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n676: \t\t\t\t}\n677: \t\t\t}\n678: \t\t}\n679: \n680: \t\t// Planned resources are temporarily stored in state with empty values,\n681: \t\t// and need to be replaced by the planned value here.\n682: \t\tif is.Current.Status == states.ObjectPlanned {\n683: \t\t\tif change == nil {\n684: \t\t\t\t// FIXME: This is usually an unfortunate case where we need to\n685: \t\t\t\t// lookup an individual instance referenced via \"self\" for\n686: \t\t\t\t// postconditions which we know exists, but because evaluation\n687: \t\t\t\t// must always get the resource in aggregate some instance\n688: \t\t\t\t// changes may not yet be registered.\n689: \t\t\t\tinstances[key] = cty.DynamicVal\n690: \t\t\t\t// log the problem for debugging, since it may be a legitimate error we can't catch\n691: \t\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"[WARN] instance %s is marked as having a change pending but that change is not recorded in the plan\", instAddr)\n692: \t\t\t\tcontinue\n693: \t\t\t}\n694: \t\t\tinstances[key] = change.After\n695: \t\t\tcontinue\n696: \t\t}\n697: \n698: \t\tios, err := is.Current.Decode(ty)\n699: \t\tif err != nil {\n700: \t\t\t// This shouldn't happen, since by the time we get here we\n701: \t\t\t// should have upgraded the state data already.\n702: \t\t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n703: \t\t\t\tSeverity: hcl.DiagError,\n704: \t\t\t\tSummary:  \"Invalid resource instance data in state\",\n705: \t\t\t\tDetail:   fmt.Sprintf(\"Instance %s data could not be decoded from the state: %s.\", instAddr, err),\n706: \t\t\t\tSubject:  &config.DeclRange,\n707: \t\t\t})\n708: \tvar diags tfdiags.Diagnostics\n792: \n793: \tif d.Operation == walkValidate || d.Operation == walkEval {\n794: \t\t// Ephemeral instances are never live during the validate walk. \t\t// module, even if the value assigned to it from outside is not. This\n195: \t\t// is a useful simplification so that module authors can be explicit\n196: \t\t// about what guarantees they are intending to make (regardless of\n197: \t\t// current implementation details). ct != 0 {\n243: \t\t\tpanic(fmt.Sprintf(\"check state says that %s should have no rules, but it has %d\", addr, ct))\n244: \t\t}\n245: \t\treturn diags\n246: \t}\n247: \n248: \t// We'll build just one evaluation context covering the data needed by\n249: \t// all of the rules together, since that'll minimize lock contention\n250: \t// on the state, plan, etc.\n251: \tscope := ctx.EvaluationScope(nil, nil, EvalDataForNoInstanceKey)\n252: \tvar refs []*addrs.Reference\n253: \tfor _, rule := range rules {\n254: \t\tcondRefs, moreDiags := langrefs.ReferencesInExpr(addrs.ParseRef, rule.Condition)\n255: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(moreDiags)\n256: \t\tmsgRefs, moreDiags := langrefs.ReferencesInExpr(addrs.ParseRef, rule.ErrorMessage)\n257: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(moreDiags)\n258: \t\trefs = append(refs, condRefs...)\n259: \t\trefs = append(refs, msgRefs...)\n260: \t}\n261: \tif diags.HasErrors() {\n262: \t\t// If any of the references were invalid then evaluating the expressions\n263: \t\t// will duplicate those errors, so we'll bail out early.\n264: \t\treturn diags\n265: \t}\n266: \thclCtx, moreDiags := scope.EvalContext(refs)\n267: \tdiags = diags.Append(moreDiags)\n268: \tif moreDiags.HasErrors() {\n269: \t\treturn diags\n270: \t}\n271: \n272: \t// HACK: Historically we manually built a very constrained hcl.EvalContext\n273: \t// here, which only included the value of the one specific input variable\n274: \t// we're validating, since we didn't yet support referring to anything\n275: \t// else. That accidentally bypassed our rule that input variables are\n276: \t// always unknown during the validate walk, and thus accidentally created\n277: \t// a useful behavior of actually checking constant-only values against\n278: \t// their validation rules just during \"terraform validate\", rather than\n279: \t// having to run \"terraform plan\".\n280: \t//\n281: \t// Although that behavior was accidental, it makes simple validation rules\n282: \t// more useful and is protected by compatibility promises, and so we'll\n283: \t// fake it here by overwriting the unknown value that scope.EvalContext\n284: \t// will have inserted with a possibly-more-known value using the same\n285: \t// strategy our special code used to use.\n286: \tourVal := ctx.NamedValues().GetInputVariableValue(addr)\n287: \tif ourVal != cty.NilVal {\n288: \t\t// (it would be weird for ourVal to be nil here, but we'll tolerate it\n289: \t\t// because it was scope.EvalContext's responsibility to check for the\n290: \t\t// absent final value, and even if it didn't we'll just get an\n291: \t\t// evaluation error when evaluating the expressions below anyway.)\n292: \n293: \t\t// Our goal here is to make sure that a reference to the variable\n294: \t\t// we're checking will evaluate to ourVal, regardless of what else\n295: \t\t// scope.EvalContext might have put in the variables table.\n296: \t\tif hclCtx.Variables == nil {\n297: \t\t\thclCtx.Variables = make(map[string]cty.Value)\n298: \t\t}\n299: \t\tif varsVal, ok := hclCtx.Variables[\"var\"]; ok {\n300: \t\t\t// Unfortunately we need to unpack and repack the object here,\n301: \t\t\t// because cty values are immutable.\n302: \t\t\tattrs := varsVal.AsValueMap()\n303: \t\t\tattrs[addr.Variable.Name] = ourVal\n304: \t\t\thclCtx.Variables[\"var\"] = cty.ObjectVal(attrs)\n305: \t\t} else {\n306: \t\t\thclCtx.Variables[\"var\"] = cty.ObjectVal(map[string]cty.Value{\n307: \t\t\t\taddr.Variable.Name: ourVal,\n308: \t\t\t})\n309: \t\t}\n310: \t}\n311: \n312: \tfor ix, validation := range rules {\n313: \t\tresult, ruleDiags := evalVariableValidation(validation, hclCtx, valueRng, addr, ix, validateWalk)\n314: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(ruleDiags)\n315: \n316: \t\tlog.Printf(\"[TRACE] evalVariableValidations: %s status is now %s\", addr, result.Status)\n317: \t\tif result.Status == checks.StatusFail {\n318: \t\t\tcheckState.ReportCheckFailure(addr, addrs.InputValidation, ix, result.FailureMessage)\n319: \t\t} else {\n320: \t\t\tcheckState.ReportCheckResult(addr, addrs.InputValidation, ix, result.Status)\n321: \t\t}\n322: \t}\n323: \n324: \treturn diags\n325: }\n326: \n327: func evalVariableValidation(validation *configs.CheckRule, hclCtx *hcl.EvalContext, valueRng hcl.Range, addr addrs.AbsInputVariableInstance, ix int, validateWalk bool) (checkResult, tfdiags.Diagnostics) {\n328: \tconst errInvalidCondition = \"Invalid variable validation result\"\n329: \tconst errInvalidValue = \"Invalid value for variable\"\n330: \tvar diags tfdiags.Diagnostics\n331: \n332: \tresult, moreDiags := validation.Condition.Value(hclCtx)\n333: \tdiags = diags.Append(moreDiags)\n334: \terrorValue, errorDiags := validation.ErrorMessage.Value(hclCtx)\n335: \n336: \t// The following error handling is a workaround to preserve backwards\n337: \t// compatibility. Due to an implementation quirk, all prior versions of\n338: \t// Terraform would treat error messages specified using JSON\n339: \t// configuration syntax (.tf.json) as string literals, even if they\n340: \t// contained the \"${\" template expression operator. This behaviour did\n341: \t// not match that of HCL configuration syntax, where a template\n342: \t// expression would result in a validation error.\n343: \t//\n344: \t// As a result, users writing or generating JSON configuration syntax\n345: \t// may have specified error messages which are invalid template\n346: \t// expressions. As we add support for error message expressions, we are\n347: \t// unable to perfectly distinguish between these two cases.\n348: \t//\n349: \t// To ensure that we don't break backwards compatibility, we have the\n350: \t// below fallback logic if the error message fails to evaluate. This\n351: \t// should only have any effect for JSON configurations. The gohcl\n352: \t// DecodeExpression function behaves differently when the source of the\n353: \t// expression is a JSON configuration file and a nil context is passed.\n354: \tif errorDiags.HasErrors() {\n355: \t\t// Attempt to decode the expression as a string literal. Passing\n356: \t\t// nil as the context forces a JSON syntax string value to be\n357: \t\t// interpreted as a string literal.\n358: \t\tvar errorString string\n359: \t\tmoreErrorDiags := gohcl.DecodeExpression(validation.ErrorMessage, nil, &errorString)\n360: \t\tif !moreErrorDiags.HasErrors() {\n361: \t\t\t// Decoding succeeded, meaning that this is a JSON syntax\n362: \t\t\t// string value. We rewrap that as a cty value to allow later\n363: \t\t\t// decoding to succeed.\n364: \t\t\terrorValue = cty.StringVal(errorString)\n365: \n366: \t\t\t// This warning diagnostic explains this odd behaviour, while\n367: \t\t\t// giving us an escape hatch to change this to a hard failure\n368: \t\t\t// in some future Terraform 1.x version.\n369: \t\t\terrorDiags = hcl.Diagnostics{\n370: \t\t\t\t&hcl.Diagnostic{\n371: \t\t\t\t\tSeverity:    hcl.DiagWarning,\n372: \t\t\t\t\tSummary:     \"Validation error message expression is invalid\",\n373: \t\t\t\t\tDetail:      fmt.Sprintf(\"The error message provided could not be evaluated as an expression, so Terraform is interpreting it as a string literal.\\n\\nIn future versions of Terraform, this will be considered an error. Please file a GitHub issue if this would break your workflow.\\n\\n%s\", errorDiags.Error()),\n374: \t\t\t\t\tSubject:     validation.ErrorMessage.Range().Ptr(),\n375: \t\t\t\t\tContext:     validation.DeclRange.Ptr(),\n376: \t\t\t\t\tExpression:  validation.ErrorMessage,\n377: \t\t\t\t\tEvalContext: hclCtx,\n378: \t\t\t\t},\n379: \t\t\t}\n380: \t\t}\n381: \n382: \t\t// We want to either report the original diagnostics if the\n383: \t\t// fallback failed, or the warning generated above if it succeeded.\n384: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(errorDiags)\n385: \t}\n386: \n387: \tif diags.HasErrors() {\n388: \t\tlog.Printf(\"[TRACE] evalVariableValidations: %s rule %s check rule evaluation failed: %s\", addr, validation.DeclRange, diags.Err().Error())\n389: \t}\n390: \tif !result.IsKnown() {\n391: \t\tlog.Printf(\"[TRACE] evalVariableValidations: %s rule %s condition value is unknown, so skipping validation for now\", addr, validation.DeclRange)\n392: \n393: \t\treturn checkResult{Status: checks.StatusUnknown}, diags // We'll wait until we've learned more, then.\n394: \t}\n395: \tif result.IsNull() {\n396: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n397: \t\t\tSeverity:    hcl.DiagError,\n398: \t\t\tSummary:     errInvalidCondition,\n399: \t\t\tDetail:      \"Validation condition expression must return either true or false, not null.\",\n400: \t\t\tSubject:     validation.Condition.Range().Ptr(),\n401: \t\t\tExpression:  validation.Condition,\n402: \t\t\tEvalContext: hclCtx,\n403: \t\t})\n404: \t\treturn checkResult{Status: checks.StatusError}, diags\n405: \t}\n406: \tvar err error\n407: \tresult, err = convert.Convert(result, cty.Bool)\n408: \tif err != nil {\n409: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n410: \t\t\tSeverity:    hcl.DiagError,\n411: \t\t\tSummary:     errInvalidCondition,\n412: \t\t\tDetail:      fmt.Sprintf(\"Invalid validation condition result value: %s.\", tfdiags.FormatError(err)),\n413: \t\t\tSubject:     validation.Condition.Range().Ptr(),\n414: \t\t\tExpression:  validation.Condition,\n415: \t\t\tEvalContext: hclCtx,\n416: \t\t})\n417: \t\treturn checkResult{Status: checks.StatusError}, diags\n418: \t}\n419: \n420: \t// Validation condition may be marked if the input variable is bound to\n421: \t// a sensitive value. This is irrelevant to the validation process, so\n422: \t// we discard the marks now.\n423: \tresult, _ = result.Unmark()\n424: \tstatus := checks.StatusForCtyValue(result)\n425: \n426: \tif status != checks.StatusFail {\n427: \t\treturn checkResult{Status: status}, diags\n428: \t}\n429: \n430: \tif !errorValue.IsKnown() {\n431: \t\tif validateWalk {\n432: \t\t\tlog.Printf(\"[DEBUG] evalVariableValidations: %s rule %s error_message value is unknown, so skipping validation for now\", addr, validation.DeclRange)\n433: \t\t\treturn checkResult{Status: checks.StatusUnknown}, diags\n434: \t\t}\n435: \n436: \t\tdiags = diags.Append(&hcl.Diagnostic{\n437: \t\t\tSeverity:    hcl.DiagError,\n438: \t\t\tSummary:     \"Invalid error message\",\n439: \t\t\tDetail:
